# Obsidian Session Review

**Purpose**: Capture the full context of your current work, progress, and learning at the end of a session.

**When to Use**:
- At end of productive work session
- When switching contexts
- Before taking a break
- Weekly reflection

**Execution Steps**:

1. Read today's daily note:
   - `10_Areas/Life/Enumerate/Daily/[current-date].md`

2. Check project status:
   - List `20_Progress/Projects/` folder
   - Read active project notes
   - Identify progress and blockers

3. Review learning notes:
   - Check `20_Progress/Degree/` for class progress
   - Check `40_Resources/` for new learning
   - Read `30_Order/Templates/Frontmatter/For Thoughts.md` for reflections

4. Check habits and goals:
   - Read `10_Areas/Life/Habits/`
   - Check weekly progress in `10_Areas/Life/Enumerate/Weekly/`

5. Generate session summary with:
   - What was accomplished
   - What was learned
   - Progress on habits/goals
   - Next session priorities

**Output Template**:

```markdown
## [Date] Session Review

### Accomplishments
- [List of completed tasks]

### Learning & Insights
- [New concepts learned]
- [Resources reviewed]

### Habit Progress
- [Habit tracking status]

### Project Status
- [Active projects with progress]

### Blockers & Questions
- [Current obstacles]

### Next Session Priorities
1. [Priority 1]
2. [Priority 2]
3. [Priority 3]
```

**Tip**: Save this via the session-archivist agent or `/second-brain-compress` to `60_Claude/07_AI_Information/Session Logs/[date]-session.md`.

Today's Obsidian Daily Review:

**Purpose**: Check in with your current state, progress, and upcoming priorities.

**Execution Steps**:

1. Get today's actual date (YYYY-MM-DD format).

2. Search the vault for recent progress and activity:

   - Read recent notes in `20_Progress/` for project status
   - Check habit board at `10_Areas/Life/Habits/Habit Tracker Board.md`

   - Read the most recently modified project notes

   - Extract open questions and next actions from the most recent log

5. Check for relevant learning:
   - List `40_Resources/` for new resources
   - Check `60_Claude/20_Distilled_Notes/` for recent evergreen notes

**Output Format**:

```markdown
## Today: [Date]
### From Your Daily Note
[Summary of notes]

### Pending / Next
[List of pending items]

### Habit Tracking
[Recent habit status]

### Project Check-in
[Active projects status]

### Learning Connections
[Relevant learning from vault]
```

**Follow-up Questions to Ask**:
- Which project should I focus on today?
- Any habits to adjust based on recent performance?
- Any learning to apply to current work?

# Vault Review

**Purpose**: Weekly or monthly review of vault content, creating summaries.

**Weekly Review Process**:
1. Read all `YYYY-MM-DD.md` notes from the week
2. Identify patterns and themes
3. Extract key learnings
4. Note incomplete tasks for rollover
5. Create summary in `60_Claude/30_Reviews/YYYY-[W]WW-summary.md`

**Monthly Review Process**:
1. Read all weekly summaries from the month
2. Identify monthly themes
3. Note progress on goals
4. Plan for next month
5. Create summary in `60_Claude/30_Reviews/YYYY-MM-summary.md`

**Template**:
```markdown
---
created: YYYY-MM-DD
type: summary
tags: [review, summary]
---

# [Weekly/Monthly] Summary - [Date Range]

## Highlights
- [Highlight 1]
- [Highlight 2]

## Key Learnings
- [Learning 1]
- [Learning 2]

## Tasks Completed
- [Task 1]
- [Task 2]

## Open Questions
- [Question 1]
- [Question 2]

## Next Steps
- [Action 1]
- [Action 2]
```

**Output**: Creates summary in `60_Claude/30_Reviews/`

  ```
# after-edit-log.ps1
# Logs every file edit to ~/.claude/session-logs/<today>-edits.md
# PostToolUse hook — must NEVER exit non-zero, it is logging-only

$ErrorActionPreference = "SilentlyContinue"

try {
    $today     = Get-Date -Format "yyyy-MM-dd"
    $timestamp = Get-Date -Format "HH:mm:ss"

    $filePath = $env:CLAUDE_FILE_PATHS
    if (-not $filePath) {
        $raw = [Console]::In.ReadToEnd()
        if ($raw) {
            try {
                $hook = $raw | ConvertFrom-Json
                $filePath = $hook.tool_input.file_path
                if (-not $filePath) { $filePath = $hook.tool_input.path }
            } catch {}
        }
    }
    if (-not $filePath) { $filePath = "(unknown)" }

    # Native Windows pwsh doesn't always populate $env:HOME the way WSL's
    # pwsh does - fall back to $env:USERPROFILE (this repo's WSL original
    # doesn't need this fallback, so it's Windows-only, not backported).
    $homeDir = $env:HOME
    if (-not $homeDir) { $homeDir = $env:USERPROFILE }
    $logDir = "$homeDir/.claude/session-logs"
    if (-not (Test-Path $logDir)) {
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $logDir -Force | Out-Null
    }

    $logFile = "$logDir/$today-edits.md"
    $entry   = "- **$timestamp** ``$filePath``"
    Add-Content -Path $logFile -Value $entry -Encoding UTF8
} catch {
    # Silently swallow all errors — never block a turn
}

exit 0

  ```
- `Write` — `/mnt/c/Users/Anant Gupta/.claude/hooks/session-wrapup.ps1`
  ```
# session-wrapup.ps1
# Prints a one-line reminder to log the session if no log exists for today
# Stop hook — must NEVER exit non-zero

$ErrorActionPreference = "SilentlyContinue"

try {
    [Console]::OutputEncoding = [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8
    $today   = Get-Date -Format "yyyy-MM-dd"
    # Native Windows pwsh doesn't always populate $env:HOME - fall back to
    # $env:USERPROFILE (Windows-only fallback, not backported to the WSL
    # original since WSL's pwsh always sets $env:HOME correctly).
    $homeDir = $env:HOME
    if (-not $homeDir) { $homeDir = $env:USERPROFILE }
    $logDir  = "$homeDir/.claude/session-logs"
    $logFile = "$logDir/$today-session.md"

    if (-not (Test-Path $logFile)) {
        Write-Host "[ session ] No log for $today — run /second-brain-compress or vault /closeday when done."
    }
} catch {
    # Silently swallow all errors
}

exit 0

  ```

As of 2026-08-10 through 2026-09-04, this was correctly empty except `skills/export-ai-session/SKILL.md` — real inspection each time confirmed `agents/`, `commands/`, `hooks/`, and `CLAUDE.md` genuinely didn't exist on the Windows side (not a sync gap). **2026-09-05: populated for the first time**, at Anant's direct request. Content mirrors the WSL home's obsidian-vault/second-brain tooling (same conceptual set — these hooks are already written in cross-platform PowerShell specifically so they'd run on either home, and the vault they operate on lives on the D: drive, reachable identically from WSL or native Windows):
- `agents/` — 3 files, copied from WSL's `obsidian-architect.md`, `obsidian-researcher.md`, `obsidian-session-archivist.md`.
- `commands/` — 7 files, copied from WSL's `obsidian-daily-review.md`, `obsidian-session-review.md`, `second-brain-capture.md`, `second-brain-compress.md`, `second-brain-graduate.md`, `second-brain-resume.md`, `second-brain-review.md`.
- `hooks/` — 2 of WSL's 3: `after-edit-log.ps1`, `session-wrapup.ps1`, each with a `$env:HOME` → `$env:USERPROFILE` fallback added (Windows PowerShell 5.1 — `pwsh`/PowerShell Core is NOT installed on this Windows machine, confirmed by checking the standard install path and `where.exe pwsh` — doesn't set `$env:HOME` the way WSL's `pwsh` does). Registered in the real `.claude/settings.json`'s `PostToolUse` (matcher `Write|Edit|MultiEdit`) and `Stop` arrays, as NEW entries alongside the existing `merget` plugin hooks (every event already has a `merget-hooks.exe record` hook wired — did not touch those). Invoked via `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File ...`, not `pwsh`, to match what's actually installed.
- **Deliberately NOT copied:** `wsl-session-export.ps1` (WSL's 3rd hook, ~700 lines). It's WSL-specific by design, not just by name — hardcoded `VaultConvRoot` pointing at `.../AI Conversations/WSL/Claude Code`, `source_os: wsl` in its own output frontmatter, reads WSL's `$env:HOME/.claude/projects`. Its own comments reference a "Windows-side design (`export-claude-session.ps1`, brought to parity 2026-08-11)" that could not be found anywhere on the real Windows filesystem (searched `C:\Users\Anant Gupta` for it directly) — so either it never actually got built, or it lives somewhere else undiscovered. Writing a ~700-line Windows equivalent blind, with no Windows pwsh environment available to test it in, was judged too risky to do as part of this pass — flagged as a real, specific, separate gap, not attempted.
- **Deliberately NOT copied:** the WSL global `CLAUDE.md` (4 lines, entirely a `/graphify` skill trigger) — graphify isn't installed in Windows's `skills/` (only `export-ai-session` + firecrawl-* plugin junctions are), so the rule wouldn't apply. Windows has no global `CLAUDE.md` as of this update; not fabricated one with no real content to put in it.

## What this means in practice
A skill or command that lives in only one home directory is not available in a session running on the other OS. Concretely today: `/second-brain-capture` and the three `obsidian-*` agents work in a WSL Claude Code session and do not exist at all in a Windows Claude Code session; `export-ai-session` is the one piece of global tooling genuinely shared in spirit across both (present as real content on Windows, and — separately — WSL's own conversation-capture hook does the equivalent job natively rather than by sharing the skill file). This is the same disjoint-content gap [[20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/Sync - Unison]] flagged as an open, undecided reconciliation question on 2026-08-10 — still open today, not resolved by this audit.
  + ## Windows home (`C:\Users\Anant Gupta\.claude`)
Thin from 2026-08-10 through 2026-09-04 — verified bare on both those dates via `Get-ChildItem`. **Populated for real on 2026-09-05**, at Anant's direct request, closing most of this section's gap:
- **Agents (3):** `obsidian-architect`, `obsidian-researcher`, `obsidian-session-archivist` — copied from WSL, same content (see WSL section above).
- **Commands (7):** `obsidian-daily-review`, `obsidian-session-review`, `second-brain-capture`, `second-brain-compress`, `second-brain-graduate`, `second-brain-resume`, `second-brain-review` — copied from WSL, same content.
- **Hooks (2 of WSL's 3):** `after-edit-log.ps1` and `session-wrapup.ps1`, each with a `$env:HOME` → `$env:USERPROFILE` fallback (Windows PowerShell 5.1 doesn't set `$env:HOME`; confirmed `pwsh`/PowerShell Core isn't installed on this machine at all). Wired into the real `.claude/settings.json`'s `PostToolUse`/`Stop` arrays as new entries alongside the pre-existing `merget` plugin hooks (every event already had a `merget-hooks.exe record` hook — untouched). `wsl-session-export.ps1` deliberately NOT ported: it's WSL-specific by design (hardcoded `VaultConvRoot` pointing at the WSL conversation-archive path, `source_os: wsl` baked into its own output, reads WSL's `$env:HOME/.claude/projects`), its own comments reference a Windows counterpart (`export-claude-session.ps1`) that could not be found anywhere on the real Windows filesystem, and authoring a ~700-line replacement blind with no Windows pwsh to test it in was judged too risky for this pass. Real, open gap — not attempted.
- **Skills:** unchanged — still exactly one real skill, `export-ai-session/SKILL.md`, plus the 31 `firecrawl-*` junctions (not part of Claude Code's own config, per the original 2026-08-20 finding below).

## What this means in practice
As of 2026-09-05, the obsidian-vault/second-brain agent+command set (and 2 of 3 hooks) are now genuinely shared across both homes — a WSL and a native-Windows Claude Code session both have `/second-brain-capture` and the three `obsidian-*` agents available. What's still genuinely disjoint: WSL's full session-export pipeline (`wsl-session-export.ps1`) has no Windows equivalent yet (see above — a real gap, not a decided non-goal); the 28 WSL skills (Cloudflare/Workers references, per-class Obsidian skills) were not evaluated for whether they belong on Windows too — this pass only handled agents/commands/hooks, per the specific request that triggered it. `export-ai-session` remains the one piece of global tooling genuinely shared in spirit from the start (present as real content on Windows; WSL does the equivalent job natively via its own hook rather than by sharing the skill file). The disjoint-content question [[20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/Sync - Unison]] flagged as open on 2026-08-10 is now partially resolved — not fully, and not by unilateral judgment about which of WSL's 28 skills belong globally on Windows too.

# Windows path conventions

- Prefer absolute paths on this machine; the working directory resolves differently across the shells in use here (native PowerShell, Git Bash, WSL), so relative paths are not reliable across tools.
- Use the drive that actually holds the target: `C:\Users\Anant Gupta` is this machine's Claude Code home and tool-config drive; `D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis` is the Jarvis vault; `D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\The Plan` is The Plan vault.
- In PowerShell, backslashes and forward slashes both work. In Bash (Git Bash), convert to `/c/Users/...` or `/d/...` form, or quote the Windows-style path as-is.
- Never assume a WSL-style path (`/mnt/c/...`) is valid from native PowerShell or Git Bash, or the reverse.

- `Edit` — `/home/anant_gupta/projects/ai/claude/second-brain-claudekit/60_Claude/scripts/sync-all.sh`
  ```diff
  -   # agents|commands|hooks|skills/<Name>/ — same one-way reference-copy
  # pattern as instructions/ above, derived from this entry's own `paths`
  # (any path whose basename is one of these four category names) rather
  # than a separate manifest field — one list stays the single source of
  # truth instead of two lists that can drift apart. Only created when the
  # real source directory exists and actually has content: an empty or
  # absent category means nothing has been decided-ready for that project
  # yet, and this deliberately doesn't invent a placeholder folder for it.
  # Symlinks (e.g. .claude_windows/skills' firecrawl-* links to an external,
  # machine-specific path) are stripped after copy — same "skip symlinks"
  # behavior Unison's `-fat` flag already applies to the paths above.
  for P in "${PATH_LIST[@]}"; do
    CATEGORY="$(basename "$P")"
    case "$CATEGORY" in
      agents|commands|hooks|skills) ;;
      *) continue ;;
    esac
  +   # agents|commands|hooks|skills|context|rules/<Name>/ — same one-way
  # reference-copy pattern as instructions/ above, derived from this entry's
  # own `paths` (any path whose basename is one of these six category names)
  # rather than a separate manifest field — one list stays the single source
  # of truth instead of two lists that can drift apart. Only created when the
  # real source directory exists and actually has content: an empty or
  # absent category means nothing has been decided-ready for that project
  # yet, and this deliberately doesn't invent a placeholder folder for it.
  # context/rules added 2026-09-05 (.claude_windows onboarding) — same
  # mechanism as the original four, not a special case; this also means
  # Jarvis's own long-listed .claude/context and .claude/rules paths start
  # populating context/Jarvis/ and rules/Jarvis/ here for the first time.
  # Symlinks (e.g. .claude_windows/skills' firecrawl-* links to an external,
  # machine-specific path) are stripped after copy — same "skip symlinks"
  # behavior Unison's `-fat` flag already applies to the paths above.
  for P in "${PATH_LIST[@]}"; do
    CATEGORY="$(basename "$P")"
    case "$CATEGORY" in
      agents|commands|hooks|skills|context|rules) ;;
      *) continue ;;
    esac
  ```

 There is a real, named, longer-horizon goal on top of both purposes: **Jarvis's own setup becoming self-improving**, using the evidence this pipeline generates. It is explicitly in scope — not a maybe — but it is strictly sequenced, and the sequence is the whole point:
 
 1. **The qualification pipeline runs solidly, for real, for a real stretch of time.** Not a proof of concept — the actual discipline in `_docs/Promotion-Criteria.md` applied repeatedly, honestly, including the "no" verdicts.
-2. **Real evidence accumulates** — dated, tested, decided rows in `20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Claude Kit/Tool Map.md`, not intentions.

(`Tool Map.md`'s own trail starts further back, at `40_Resources/CS/Repos.md` — the master ~95-repo starred index, tiered `Implement > Knowledge`, where every repo gets a real decision marker — `(*INSTALL: QUEUED*)`/`(*SKIP*)`/`(*EVAL: DATE*)`/`(*DEFERRED*)` — before it's ever cloned into `sandbox/`. Every per-repo "why" behind a `Tool Map.md` row ultimately traces back to this file; see `_docs/Jarvis.md`'s citation of the same file for the full chain.)
 3. **Only then does anyone decide what "self-improving" concretely automates.** Nothing here pre-specifies the mechanism. Deciding the mechanism before step 1 and 2 produce real evidence would be exactly the "plan-and-never-run" failure mode `_docs/PRD.md` names, aimed at a bigger target.
 4. **Whatever gets built is small and logged, never silent** — the same non-negotiable rule `Jarvis OS — North Star.md` already applies to its own hooks: every automatic action writes one visible line to a log. An automation that improves Jarvis invisibly is not a feature, it's the same failure mode as the `50_Claude`/sync-launcher bug found and fixed 2026-08-09 (`_docs/Repo-Map.md`) — a process that silently stopped doing its job while reporting success.

 ### `20_Progress/Projects/AI Use/Claude Kit/` — this repo's decision tracker
 
+- **`40_Resources/CS/Repos.md`** (Jarvis, upstream of this whole folder — cited here because everything below traces back to it) — the master ~95-repo GitHub-stars index, organized by star list, tiered `Implement > Knowledge`, each entry annotated `(*INSTALL: QUEUED*)`/`(*SKIP*)`/`(*EVAL: DATE*)`/`(*DEFERRED*)`. This is where the `sandbox/` → `tested-tools/` → `Promotion-Criteria.md` decision trail actually starts: a repo only gets a `Tool Map.md` row once it's been triaged here and cloned into `sandbox/`. Every real decision's per-repo "why" in `Tool Map.md` ultimately traces back to this file's markers — and, one layer further back, to the Implement>Knowledge principle itself, sourced from `PDF's Ingestion Implementation.md`'s Matrix (see the `Sources - Plan/` section below).
 - **`Tool Map.md`** — the living, per-tool ingestion record for this repo. One row per tool, updated the same session its pipeline stage changes. **As of 2026-08-09 this is more current than this repo's own docs used to be** — it already has ECC's real test results (3378/3388 tests passing) and the 17-repo 2026-07-30 sandbox batch. Per Jarvis's own "one fact, one home" principle (`Jarvis OS — North Star.md`), **`Tool Map.md` is the sole source of truth for tool-by-tool pipeline state** — this repo's own docs point here instead of keeping a second, driftable copy (see `_docs/PRD.md`).
 - **`Log.md`** — one dated entry every time a `Tool Map.md` row changes, `## [YYYY-MM-DD] tag | title` heading, following `60_Claude/07_AI_Information/Session Logs/log.md`'s convention.
 - **`Toolkit/`** — moved here from `40_Resources/CS/AI/Toolkit/` during the 2026-08 reorganization (confirmed: that old path no longer exists). Holds `Agents/`, `Commands/`, `Hooks/`, `MCPs/`, `Skills/` subfolders and a `Claude Code.md` reference note — a catalog of what's available/known, distinct from `Tool Map.md`'s pipeline-stage tracking. `Claude Code.md` states its own job precisely: "`Tool Map` answers 'is this tool trustworthy yet'; the Toolkit answers 'given a real task right now, what do I actually type.'"
